# 42: Chapter 42: Pikachu Vs. Drapion

"Wow, this Pokémon looks really fierce... huh?"

Ash looked at Drapion in awe and then took out his Pokédex to check its information.

However...

"No data available."

Seeing this message on his Pokédex, Ash was momentarily stunned.

"Your Pokédex hasn't been updated, has it? This is a Drapion from the Sinnoh region. If your Pokédex is only for the Kanto region, it won't be able to scan it."

Ilene smiled and handed her Pokédex to Ash.

Pokédexes vary by region. If it hasn't been upgraded to the National Dex, a regional Pokédex can only scan Pokémon from its specific region.

"If you're from Pallet Town, you should have Professor Oak upgrade it, or you could pay someone to do it for you."

"Oh... I didn't know that," Ash scratched his head.

'Beep~ Drapion, a Poison/Dark-type Pokémon, the Scorpion Pokémon.

Ability: Sniper

Moves: Cross Poison, Toxic Spikes, Night Slash, Earthquake, Rock Slide, etc.

Description: It has a very strong physique and can overwhelm opponents with its powerful strength, even without relying on its potent poison.'

This Drapion looks really strong!

"Poison and Dark-type... That's a really good type combination," Brock mused, supporting his chin with his hand.

"Of course, it is! The only major weakness is to Ground-type moves, so it's definitely a tough opponent," Leah casually remarked, curiously observing Drapion. Although Drapion wasn't particularly tall, standing at about 1.5 meters, its overall body length was around three meters, and its upper body could rear up, making it visually imposing, almost like a medium-large Pokémon.

"What's really important is the Toxic Spikes on the field, which were set up earlier by Arbok," Ilene remarked with a polite smile.

"Toxic Spikes?"

Ash and Misty looked at Ilene in confusion, while Leah and Brock seemed deep in thought.

"You'll see soon enough," Ilene smiled without saying more. Toxic Spikes wasn't a widely known strategy at this point, and even Koga, the Fuchsia City Gym Leader who specialized in Poison-types, rarely used it.

As a result, Toxic Spikes wasn't something most people were familiar with.

After all, there are hundreds of moves that Pokémon can learn, but only a fraction are commonly used in battles.

In many cases, a battle could be won using just a move or two.

But today, Toxic Spikes was sure to leave an impression!

...

"Drapion, Pin Missile!"

Dylan initiated the attack. Drapion's pincers on either side of its head glowed white and swung heavily.

A barrage of tiny white light beams shot through the air towards Pikachu, covering a wide area. From Pikachu's perspective, the Pin Missile barrage filled its entire field of vision.

Bug-type Pin Missile!

"Pikachu, jump up and use Thunderbolt!"

Red commanded, his voice calm. He was concerned about the Toxic Spikes; if he remembered correctly, it was a Poison-type control move.

Although Red was a prodigious trainer, having only started his journey less than six months ago, his experience against a wide range of opponents was still limited. Each battle allowed him to grow, but Dylan was the first strong Poison-type trainer he had faced, even stronger than Koga.

Pikachu leaped gracefully into the air, its cheeks sparking with electricity.

Thunderbolt!

Golden electricity shot out to meet the incoming Pin Missile barrage.

Boom!

Thunderbolt and Pin Missile collided intensely, golden electricity clashing wildly with white beams, causing violent explosions and thick smoke to fill the air.

Although Pin Missile's initial power wasn't strong, its barrage nature made it formidable once enough missiles were fired.

Strong winds and shockwaves spread out, blowing Red and Dylan's hair and clothes.

"This Drapion is stronger than that Arbok from earlier," Red narrowed his crimson eyes, confirming his suspicion.

There was no doubt; from just this one Pin Missile, he could tell that Drapion's combat power was superior to Arbok's and would be much harder to defeat.

Red considered his strategy, but the next moment...

As the Thunderbolt ended and Pikachu landed, a strange deep purple poison light spread across Pikachu's body.

"Pika..."

Pikachu winced in pain, its entire body suffused with a menacing purple glow.

This was the effect of the Toxic Spikes!

"What is this?!!"

Seeing this, everyone around was surprised.

"Pikachu!" Red's expression changed slightly.

Was it the Toxic Spikes?

"Drapion, Pin Missile!" Dylan's lips curved into a small smile.

"Long~!"

Drapion let out a deep growl, and its large pincers swung again.

A dense barrage of white Pin Missile beams shot out like arrows.

"Pikachu! Dodge it!"

Red commanded without hesitation.

Although Bug-type Pin Missile wasn't super effective against Pikachu, Pikachu's defense wasn't great, and getting hit would deal significant damage.

Pikachu gritted its teeth, enduring the pain from the Toxic Spikes as it struggled to stay on its feet, continuously retreating.

Boom! Boom! Boom!

Pin Missile blasts exploded around Pikachu, but it managed to dodge most of them, the effects of the Toxic Spikes slowly fading away.

"Volt Tackle!"

The Toxic Spikes continued to linger on the battlefield, and Red suspected they might activate again, so he needed to finish this battle quickly.

"Pika Pika Pika!!"

Pikachu dashed forward, its entire body crackling with intense electricity as it charged towards Drapion with unstoppable momentum.

Electric-type Volt Tackle! A signature move that only the Pichu evolution line could learn!

"Drapion, Night Slash!"

Drapion let out a menacing growl, its pincers glowing with a dark purple light as it advanced towards the charging Pikachu.

Dark-type Night Slash!

Pikachu, having reached Drapion, leaped up and slammed into Drapion's head with full force.

Night Slash vs. Volt Tackle!

The two glowing purple pincers clashed heavily with the Volt Tackle.

Bang!!

A dull sound echoed, and the next moment, powerful shockwaves erupted from the point of impact between the two Pokémon.

Scattered electricity shot out, the golden and black lights dazzling.

But the struggle didn't last long. Drapion's Night Slash was slowly overwhelmed by Pikachu's Volt Tackle.

Volt Tackle was incredibly powerful!

Drapion resisted with all its might, but it couldn't withstand the impact of Volt Tackle. Night Slash was shattered, and Volt Tackle slammed into Drapion's face.

Drapion roared in pain, nearly knocked over by the small Pikachu, staggering back several steps before regaining its balance.

This hit from Volt Tackle was not light.

"So cool!"

Ash exclaimed excitedly, his eyes sparkling. As expected, Pikachu is strong! Even such a big scorpion can't stand up to Pikachu!

"Strong, but what now?" Dylan raised an eyebrow, looking at Pikachu with a half-smile.

Pikachu, who had hit Drapion, was thrown back by the recoil and landed, but as it did, an electric current ran through its body—a side effect of Volt Tackle.

But that wasn't all. The ground beneath Pikachu glowed purple, and the eerie deep purple poison light once again enveloped Pikachu.

"Pika...chu..."

Pikachu winced in pain, curling up tightly as the combined effects of the Volt Tackle recoil and the Toxic Spikes took their toll.

"..." Red frowned slightly. As he feared, the Toxic Spikes triggered more than once.

"Drapion!"

Dylan seized the opportunity.

Drapion growled lowly, its tail suddenly elongating and snapping forward like lightning to grab Pikachu.

The tail moved too fast, and Pikachu, still reeling from the recoil and poison, couldn't react in time and was caught.

"Pikachu!"

Red's red eyes narrowed.

Drapion slowly lifted its tail, holding Pikachu aloft while its pincers waved menacingly.

Pikachu struggled to break free, but Drapion's tail was incredibly strong, holding Pikachu tightly.

"Pikachu! Use Thunderbolt!"

"Stop it! Use Cross Poison!"

Pikachu's cheeks began to spark, but Drapion was ready.

Its pincers glowed with purple light as it crossed them and slashed heavily, sending a cross-shaped purple light blade crashing into Pikachu.

Bang!

Cross Poison hit Pikachu perfectly, causing it to wince in pain as the electricity ceased.

Red's frown deepened; Thunderbolt had been interrupted!

"Drapion!"

The next moment, Drapion slammed Pikachu into the ground.

Its tail, still gripping Pikachu, smashed it hard into the ground, with such force that the ground seemed to tremble.

Pikachu was in visible pain. Even though it wasn't a named move, the sheer force of the slam was painful.

But in the next second, the ground beneath Pikachu glowed with that menacing purple light again, the third time the Toxic Spikes activated!

"Pika...!"

Pikachu let out a cry of agony as the Toxic Spikes invaded its system once more.

"This..."

Ash and the others were stunned!

"Earthquake!"

Drapion finally released Pikachu, but then its tail swung down heavily.

Ground-type Earthquake!

Boom! Boom! Boom!

The battlefield shook violently as a shockwave of seismic energy rippled outward.

Pikachu, being closest, took the full brunt of the attack.

Pikachu, already struggling to stand, collapsed in pain, the Earthquake being too much.

"Pikachu!"

Red's expression darkened. Pikachu couldn't hold out.

"Pikachu is unable to battle!"

The referee raised the red flag and declared the result.

The entire backyard of the Pokemon Center fell silent.

---

---

---

# 43: Chapter 43: "It's So Cool!"

"This Toxic Spikes... That's just too much, isn't it?"

"Damn! That Drapion is so cool! It's just so incredibly cool! I want to catch one too."

"Pikachu got totally wrecked, taken out in one move with no chance to fight back."

"These two are both so strong, I wonder how the next person will deal with Drapion."

The onlookers were whispering among themselves, keeping their voices low to avoid disturbing the battle between the two trainers.

As a trainer, there's no one who dislikes battles; even watching others battle is fascinating.

Watching more, learning more, practicing more—that's how you improve.

"Pikachu is unable to battle, Drapion wins!"

Leah quickly recovered, observed Pikachu, and announced the result.

"What a fierce Drapion!"

Misty couldn't help but be amazed. Wow, that was really a near-instant takedown of Pikachu by capitalizing on the Toxic Spikes!

Ash was still a bit stunned. Just a moment ago, he was praising Pikachu, and then Pikachu got caught, and... that was it.

"Indeed, Drapion is very strong, but the key here is the Toxic Spikes."

Brock nodded, then pondered for a moment before speaking.

"Toxic Spikes don't just trigger once. This puts Red at a severe disadvantage. Even though it won't poison them, the damage from Toxic Spikes can't be ignored either."

The damage from a single trigger of Toxic Spikes might not be high, but the pain from the corrosive spikes is very real, making it easy for the opponent to find an opening.

Pikachu was caught by Drapion because of the combined pain from its Volt Tackle's recoil and the Toxic Spikes' corrosion.

Otherwise, Drapion wouldn't have been able to catch the agile Pikachu so easily.

"There are ways to deal with Toxic Spikes. Poison-type Pokémon aren't affected by them, and moves like Haze or Rapid Spin can clear the field. No wonder Arbok didn't use Toxic Spikes at the start."

"Dylan is really impressive~"

Brock couldn't help but admire, but will the challenger really be happy with a battle like this?

Hmm, probably. After all, Dylan is also honing their experience in battling Poison-type trainers... right?

...

On the battlefield.

"Thank you, Pikachu."

Red gently comforted Pikachu as he recalled it, then looked up at the menacing Drapion across from him.

"Blastoise!"

Without much hesitation, Red threw out his third Poké Ball.

With a deep roar, the heavily injured Blastoise landed in front of Red once again.

But as soon as it touched the ground, the Toxic Spikes' damage hit.

Blastoise's face twisted in pain. It was already severely injured, and now the Toxic Spikes' damage made it even worse.

"So you're opting to clear the Toxic Spikes, huh?"

Dylan chuckled lightly at this.

But it makes sense. After suffering from the Toxic Spikes, Red understands how difficult it would be to battle without dealing with them first.

Although Venusaur wouldn't be poisoned, it's tough for Venusaur to battle Poison-type Pokémon. The reason is simple: both of its STAB types are not very effective against Poison types. How could it win?

"Rapid Spin."

"Pin Missile!"

Blastoise immediately retracted its limbs and head, spinning its shell rapidly.

But Dylan wasn't just going to watch. Drapion swung its pincers forcefully, launching a barrage of Pin Missiles.

Blastoise spun rapidly, skimming the ground as it moved. With Blastoise's rapid spinning, streams of purple fluid flew off the ground.

The Toxic Spikes were quickly cleared away by Rapid Spin.

But the Pin Missiles were already coming.

Boom! Boom! Boom!

The Pin Missiles struck Blastoise repeatedly, causing explosions and sending shockwaves rippling outwards.

When the bombardment ended, Blastoise was lying on the ground, unconscious.

Even though Blastoise's shell provided high resistance to physical attacks, it couldn't completely negate the damage.

Blastoise was in such poor condition that it couldn't hold on.

"Blastoise is unable to battle, Drapion wins."

Leah stepped in again to announce the battle's result.

"..."

Red recalled the fainted Blastoise, lowering the brim of his hat slightly.

"Red, even though the Toxic Spikes have been cleared, my Drapion is still tough to deal with."

Dylan smiled at the silent Red across from him. Besides, now that Blastoise is down, there's no need to worry if he uses Toxic Spikes again!

"Snorlax!"

Red remained silent, throwing out his third Poké Ball.

"Snor~"

With a flash of white light, a large, bear-like Pokémon heavily landed in front of Red.

Its body was mostly blue, with a white belly. It had a very chubby physique, with a plump face and eyes that were almost always squinting, giving it a somewhat dopey look.

It was Snorlax!

'Beep~ Snorlax, Normal type, the Drowsing Pokémon.

Ability: Thick Fat.

Moves: Belly Drum, Hammer Arm, High Horsepower, Amnesia, Heavy Slam, Giga Impact, Fire Punch...

Description: It spends its days eating and sleeping in a continuous cycle. Its gentle nature makes it a perfect playground for children on its big belly.'

Ash read the Pokédex entry with great interest, his first impression of Snorlax being very favorable.

Hmm, I'll definitely catch one in the future!

"Going for close combat, huh?" Dylan sized up the Snorlax and couldn't help but smile. He wasn't afraid of close combat.

"Snorlax, Belly Drum!"

Red glanced at Drapion and then decisively gave the order.

Snorlax roared, its body undergoing rapid changes, swelling and contracting as if enduring some kind of intense pain, its expression twisted.

A Normal-type Belly Drum!

"Going all out, huh?"

The crowd watching was stunned.

Belly Drum is a very interesting move. After using it, the user's Attack stat is maximized!

According to Pokémon researchers, a Pokémon's stat boosts have a limit, with six stages being the maximum. Any further attempts to boost the stat will have no effect.

The same goes for stat reductions, with six stages being the minimum.

Moves like Swords Dance and Nasty Plot only raise Attack and Special Attack by two stages each, but Belly Drum is different. It raises Attack by twelve stages!

This means that even if your Attack has been lowered by six stages, Belly Drum can still max out your Attack—up to twelve stages.

Of course, since six stages is the maximum boost for Attack, Belly Drum will only raise it by six stages if it hasn't been lowered.

But Belly Drum has a severe drawback: it causes significant damage to the user. In game terms, the user loses half of its HP.

"Toxic Spikes."

Dylan was a bit surprised too. Starting with Belly Drum means Red is really going all out and wants to win badly.

After Belly Drum, Snorlax's attack power is maxed out. Even a basic tackle would deal massive damage to Drapion.

But if you're boosting, I'm laying down Toxic Spikes!

Drapion's pincers gathered, forming a deep purple energy ball that was sent skyward, where it exploded. The eerie purple fluid rained down and disappeared into the battlefield.

Red watched silently. There was no other choice. After two battles, he had a rough understanding of Dylan's strategy.

It's either Toxic or Toxic Spikes...

To defeat Dylan, Red's only option was to win quickly, overwhelming the opponent's Pokémon with sheer power!

By now, Snorlax had finished using Belly Drum. Its body returned to normal, and a thick white light was absorbed into Snorlax's body, causing its aura to surge dramatically.

"Snor~!"

Snorlax raised its chubby arms, striking a powerful pose. It felt like it was overflowing with limitless, inexhaustible strength!

It felt invincible!

Snorlax tried its best to open its squinted eyes and glare at Drapion, but it was difficult. Still, no matter.

Just a mere scorpion; how could it match the might of Emperor Snorlax?

Victory was certain!

(End of Chapter)

# 44: Chapter 44: You Expect a Poison-Type User to Play Fair?

"Belly Drum succeeded!"

The audience was in awe as they watched Snorlax, now radiating an overwhelmingly powerful aura.

In official battles, it's rare for a trainer to fully maximize their Pokémon's stats. But now, Red's Snorlax had done it!

"But the Toxic Spikes have been laid down again... and Snorlax has lost a lot of health using Belly Drum. If it can't land an attack on Drapion..."

Misty furrowed her delicate brows, analyzing the situation rationally.

Snorlax's physical attack power was now absurdly high, but Pokémon battles aren't just about comparing attack stats.

And let's not forget, Snorlax had already lost a significant amount of health before even starting the fight, all because of Belly Drum.

"Red's definitely got this!" Ash declared, full of faith in his brother. After all, Red had been his idol since childhood.

"You're right, though. It all depends on how well they command their Pokémon now."

Leah nodded in agreement with Misty's assessment. Though her skills as a trainer didn't match up to Dylan's or Red's, she was still far more experienced than Ash and his friends.

...

On the battlefield.

Drapion and Snorlax, both massive Pokémon, stared each other down from a distance, the tension between them palpable.

"Drapion, Pin Missile!"

Dylan was the first to act.

With a low growl, Drapion swung its massive pincers, unleashing a barrage of white, needle-like projectiles.

Snorlax is a pure Normal-type Pokémon, which is quite unique. Among the eighteen types, only Fighting-type is super effective against it, while Ghost-type attacks are completely ineffective. Everything else deals neutral damage.

Dylan didn't know Snorlax's ability, but it had to be one of three: Immunity, Thick Fat, or Gluttony.

(Note: As mentioned before, in official battles, Pokédexes are locked from showing abilities and moves during the battle, so earlier references to the Pokédex were from Ash and his friends using their own devices. Dylan doesn't know Snorlax's ability.)

Immunity would be the most troublesome, as it prevents the Pokémon from being poisoned.

Although a single layer of Toxic Spikes won't poison a Pokémon, the corrosive damage it causes is different from regular poison damage.

Poison-type Pokémon aren't immune to the damage from Toxic Spikes, but they do resist it. However, Dylan had previously tested that Poison-types don't take any damage from a single layer of Toxic Spikes, meaning they are completely immune to it.

So, there's a chance that Snorlax, if it has the Immunity ability, could be immune to the corrosive damage from a single layer of Toxic Spikes.

Dylan wasn't entirely sure, but it was necessary to find out. Laying down the Toxic Spikes was a way to test Snorlax's ability.

If Snorlax was immune to the Toxic Spikes, then it definitely had the Immunity ability!

But if it wasn't, it was likely to have either the Thick Fat or Gluttony ability. Either way, Dylan could still rely on the Toxic Spikes to wear Snorlax down!

Even though Snorlax's attack power was through the roof, it had lost a lot of health due to Belly Drum, leaving it in a less-than-ideal state.

"Snorlax, Fire Punch!"

Red lowered the brim of his hat and gave the command in a deep voice.

Snorlax stood its ground, its chubby fist igniting in flames, which it then swung with all its might at the incoming Pin Missile.

Boom!

The flames erupted violently, swallowing up all the Pin Missiles in their path.

The red flames painted the sky, reflecting in everyone's eyes.

What a powerful Fire Punch!

"Body Slam!"

Even before the flames had completely dissipated, Red immediately issued another command after shattering the Pin Missile.

Snorlax took a heavy step, preparing to charge at Drapion. But as it stepped forward, a sinister purple light enveloped Snorlax's entire body, covering its bulky frame.

The Toxic Spikes had kicked in!

Snorlax groaned in pain, the corrosive effect of the Toxic Spikes clearly taking its toll.

"Yikes!" Ash gasped at the sight.

"Those Toxic Spikes are really nasty!"

Ash didn't like this kind of battle. He preferred intense, all-out confrontations where both sides went at each other with everything they had—his ideal version of a Pokémon battle!

To him, the underhanded control tactics of Toxic Spikes felt like cheating!

"Yeah, it's a completely unfair control tactic."

Brock nodded in agreement. Of course, as a seasoned trainer, Brock didn't think there was anything wrong with using Toxic Spikes in battle; he was just amazed at how ruthless this move was!

How could such a strong move have gone unnoticed for so long?!

If you don't have moves like Haze or Rapid Spin to clear the field, and you can't finish the battle quickly, facing Toxic Spikes feels like a death sentence!

"It really is one of the best strategies for overcoming a stronger opponent," Brock thought to himself in amazement.

Of course, Brock didn't know that it wasn't just Haze and Rapid Spin that could clear Toxic Spikes...

The real world is full of surprises. Brock would never have guessed that the clueless guy next to him would one day have his Infernape dig underground to use Flare Blitz, using the intense heat to evaporate the Toxic Spikes from the entire battlefield.

Ash was stunned. "That's just cheating!"

But that's the reality—battle strategies can't always follow game mechanics to the letter. It's like how Sandstorm, triggered by Tyranitar's Sand Stream ability, was once dispelled by a splash of water from that same clueless guy's Pikachu in the Kalos League finals against Alain. Poor Sandstorm had no dignity left.

Who would dare run a Sandstorm team now? Does that mean Surf could wipe out a Sandstorm too? After all, Surf's wave is way bigger than that splash of water.

On the battlefield.

Seeing Snorlax writhing in pain from the Toxic Spikes, Red remained silent. This was something he had to face.

"So it doesn't have the Immunity ability." Dylan squinted slightly, watching Snorlax struggle through the fading flames.

With that confirmed, Snorlax must have either Thick Fat or Gluttony as its ability.

Nothing to worry about now, Dylan thought with a slight smile.

"Drapion, Earthquake!"

The corrosive effect of the Toxic Spikes was relatively short-lived, especially for Pokémon with high endurance and willpower.

So, they needed to strike while they had the chance.

Drapion's face twisted into a snarl, its massive tail rising high before slamming down onto the ground.

A Ground-type Earthquake!

Boom!

The battlefield shook violently as the seismic shockwave surged forward.

"Body Slam!"

But Red's training was exceptional. Snorlax managed to endure the corrosive effects of the Toxic Spikes, then charged forward and leapt high into the air, limbs spread wide as it aimed at Drapion.

A Normal-type Body Slam!

Visually, this was quite a sight. Snorlax, looking like a massive, three-hundred-pound giant, actually jumped pretty high!

Some of the spectators' eyes widened—they had never seen a Snorlax jump that high before!

"Drapion!"

Dylan remained calm.

Drapion quickly retreated, avoiding Snorlax's Body Slam. Getting hit by that would be no joke.

Although Drapion was large, unlike Snorlax, it was quite fast.

Boom!

Snorlax crashed into the ground with a thud, the impact causing the entire battlefield to tremble, and a thick cloud of dust rose into the air.

"High Horsepower!" Red commanded without hesitation.

"Snor~!"

From within the dust cloud, a massive shadow emerged, surrounded by a yellowish-brown energy as it charged forward with unstoppable momentum—a truly terrifying sight!

It was Snorlax.

Ground-type High Horsepower!

"Cross Poison!"

Dylan gave the order immediately.

Drapion continued to retreat, its pincers crossing as they glowed with a purple light before unleashing a cross-shaped energy blade.

Bang!

The Poison-type Cross Poison struck Snorlax, but Snorlax barreled through it without slowing down, continuing its charge!

Dylan's eye twitched slightly. What a powerful High Horsepower!

"Go, Snorlax!" Ash shouted excitedly as Snorlax charged forward.

"If that High Horsepower lands, Drapion will definitely be knocked out," Brock said, not daring to blink.

As long as it hits. If High Horsepower connects, Drapion won't be able to withstand it.

High Horsepower is a Ground-type move, super effective against Drapion, and with Snorlax's current attack power, even if Drapion had two lives, it wouldn't be enough to survive.

Dylan understood this too, but instead of worry, a smile spread across his face.

Snorlax isn't fast, even while using High Horsepower, and High Horsepower isn't a priority move.

"Red, as an excellent trainer, you should know that sometimes, facing a strong opponent who relies on brute force isn't as difficult as it seems."

Dylan smiled at Red.

Red's crimson eyes narrowed slightly.

"It should be almost here." Dylan grinned.

"Drapion, Protect."

Now nearly backed into a corner, Drapion growled as it halted, a green barrier forming around it.

Protect, the ultimate defense! It can block almost any move!

Boom!

Snorlax's High Horsepower slammed into the Protect barrier with incredible force, sending shockwaves rippling out like a storm.

The gusts rustled Dylan's clothes and made his hair flutter, but he calmly and elegantly adjusted his collar.

Soon, the power of High Horsepower dissipated, but the thin Protect barrier remained as solid as ever.

Sizzle~

The Protect barrier's effect ended, slowly turning into light particles and fading away.

Just then, a deep purple Poison Jab struck, suddenly "engulfing" Snorlax entirely.

Snorlax's chubby face twisted in pain—this was the second wave of toxic spikes!

"Now, it's my turn to counterattack," Dylan said, his smile bright and sunny.

Substitute + Toxic Spikes, just as effective in a different form, right? After all, it's not easy to directly inflict toxic poisoning in real battles, but the poison from Toxic Spikes is just too convenient.

The first experience with a Toxic Spikes tactic is always like this, especially when Red's Blastoise has already been incapacitated and can't clear the field—it just feels so unfair, but...

You think Toxic Spikes are unfair? What fairness can you expect from someone who uses poison?

"Drapion."

In front of Snorlax, the sinister-looking Drapion slowly raised its head, its two terrifying pincers glowing with a purple poison, and its tail scorpion claw was also raised high, aiming at Snorlax.

At this moment, Drapion looked at Snorlax as if it were a helpless lamb ready for slaughter.

Not again!

Red's crimson eyes narrowed as he pressed his lips together tightly—once more, his Snorlax was being corroded by Toxic Spikes!

Always calm and composed during battles, Red felt his emotions begin to shift subtly.

(End of Chapter)

# 45: Chapter 45: It's Not My Fault, It's Dylan's!

"This..."

The onlookers were speechless. After all, this was a Pokémon that had just used Belly Drum! In battles between evenly matched opponents, a Pokémon that has used Belly Drum is nearly unbeatable. Shouldn't it be unleashing a massive show of power and devastation?

"Drapion!"

Dylan called out to Drapion.

"Draaaap!"

Drapion let out a low growl, and its pincers on its raised tail sliced through the air, clamping down on Snorlax's neck and slamming it heavily onto the ground.

Snorlax, who had just barely recovered from the effects of Toxic Spikes, had no time to react and was immediately pinned to the ground by Drapion's tail.

"Cross Poison!"

Drapion's two pincers crossed and then swung down heavily, sending a sharp Cross Poison attack shooting towards Snorlax.

Bang!

Snorlax, who was just about to struggle to get up, was hit squarely, letting out a pained cry as its movements came to a halt.

Although Snorlax is bulky and fat, its physical defense is actually quite low—one of the lowest, in fact. However, its stamina is incredibly high.

But Snorlax had already used Belly Drum, and now its condition was far from good.

"Snorlax, grab it!"

Red's expression tightened as he commanded in a deep voice.

Snorlax isn't Pikachu! Right now, Snorlax's strength is far beyond Drapion's!

Snorlax, enduring the pain, reached out its large, chubby hand to grab Drapion's tail that was locked around its neck.

"Earthquake!"

But it wasn't going to be that easy. Drapion quickly retracted its tail. If Snorlax had grabbed it, it could have swung Drapion around and smashed it to the ground without mercy.

Snorlax missed its target, but Drapion wasn't done yet.

Its raised tail brought down a fierce gust of wind as it slammed into the ground next to Snorlax.

"Snorlax!" Red's brows furrowed deeply.

Rumble!

The ground trembled violently as the solidified shockwaves of the Earthquake rippled out, hitting the nearby Snorlax directly.

Snorlax let out a pained roar; the damage from Earthquake was not insignificant!

"Snorlax has to stand up! If it doesn't, it will keep taking hits!"

Misty was getting anxious.

"Impossible. Dylan won't give it a chance," Brock shook his head.

"Plus... Toxic Spikes will probably come into play again!"

But the Toxic Spikes might not even be necessary.

On the battlefield, Snorlax, who had fallen to the ground, was already severely weakened. Its injuries were serious.

"Snorlax, use Rest!" Red quickly thought of a solution and immediately ordered Snorlax.

"Not a chance!" Dylan's eyes flashed.

"Cross Poison!"

Snorlax closed its eyes, preparing to enter the Rest state. Rest is a Normal-type move that allows the user to recover health as long as it sleeps.

But Drapion's pincers were already swinging, and Cross Poison was rapidly approaching.

Boom!

Cross Poison exploded against Snorlax's body, spreading thick dust that obscured it from view.

"Did it work? Did Snorlax succeed?"

"I don't know. Cross Poison came too quickly. Snorlax might not have had time to use Rest, right?"

"Not necessarily. Rest is fast. As long as it fell asleep, Snorlax's health would recover, and it could withstand Cross Poison."

"Why is the dust still not clearing?!"

The spectators were all drawn in by the intense battle, each more anxious than the last.

Ash and his friends felt the same way, especially Ash, who watched the gradually dissipating dust with bated breath.

Did it succeed? If Snorlax managed to fall asleep, there was a chance it could turn the battle around!

Red also stared intently at the dust, focusing on Snorlax...

Slowly, Snorlax's form became visible to the crowd.

But when they saw Snorlax, Red's eyes flickered slightly, and he remained silent.

Snorlax's eyes had turned to swirls, indicating it had fainted. In the end, Cross Poison was just a bit too fast, and Snorlax hadn't managed to use Rest.

"Snorlax is unable to battle. Drapion wins!"

Leah stepped forward to check and crisply announced the result.

On the scoreboard, Snorlax's portrait on Red's side dimmed. This was a 4v4 battle, and Red now only had one Pokémon left.

Red silently recalled Snorlax, staring at its Pokéball for a long moment, his emotions complex.

To be honest, by this point in the battle, Red understood that his disadvantage was now significant.

After all, Drapion was only Dylan's second Pokémon, and it was still in good condition. This meant that if Red wanted to win, his last Pokémon would have to defeat all three of Dylan's remaining Pokémon.

There was no denying it—Dylan was different from any opponent Red had faced before. In his previous battles, almost all of them were straightforward, face-to-face confrontations.

But Dylan was different. While his Pokémon weren't weak, the true core of his team seemed to be the poison and Toxic Spikes field strategy he employed. At least, that's how Red saw it for now.

Even though Dylan had only used two Pokémon so far, Red knew that whether it was Arbok or the stronger Drapion, in a normal, straightforward battle, he believed his Blastoise and Pikachu could defeat them. But now? Even Snorlax had been taken down by Drapion.

This was the first time Red had faced an opponent like this—he had really learned something.

"There's still a chance!" But Red never gave up. He took a deep breath and steadied his mind. As long as the battle wasn't over, there was still a chance to turn the tables!

He couldn't give up!

"Dylan is really strong, isn't he?"

Ash also realized his brother's situation was becoming difficult. He stayed silent for a while before finally squeezing out this comment.

He really hadn't expected that his older brother, whom he admired, would be at such a disadvantage against someone his own age.

Dylan now had three match points in hand!

Ilene's fair face showed an even deeper smile, clearly indicating she was in a very good mood.

"Ilene?"

"What's up, Leah?"

"Dylan really started changing just half a month ago?" Leah couldn't help but fall into deep thought, suddenly realizing something.

"Yes." Ilene was taken aback, then nodded.

"It was around that time that he decided to use a Poison-type team first. Most of the Pokémon like Arbok, Drapion, and Sprigatito were captured and trained by Dylan during this period."

Ilene smiled as she spoke.

Ilene wasn't a trainer herself, although she knew that for Dylan to have trained Pokémon like Arbok to be so strong in just half a month, he must be an incredibly talented trainer.

But she didn't have a clear concept of how remarkable that was.

Hearing this, Leah, Misty, and Brock were stunned, while Ash didn't think too much about it.

"Can you be more specific? Were Pokémon like Arbok already strong when he caught them?"

Misty couldn't help but ask, her nerves growing tense.

Are you kidding? Captured and trained in just half a month? Half a month!! And they're already this strong?!

"Well... not really. Skorupi was sent to Dylan by his father, but back then, Arbok was still just an Ekans. I went with Dylan to Mount Moon, and back then, Arbok couldn't even beat a Floragato. It was really weak."

Ilene spoke gently and nostalgically, but then noticed the bizarre expressions on Misty, Brock, and Leah's faces.

"Huh? What's wrong with you guys?"

"Heh heh heh... I really am a failure..."

Misty let out a dry laugh, her eyes hollow as she finally gave up all hope.

Even Red took three or four months of travel to reach his current level of strength!

What is Dylan trying to do? Is he trying to drive all the other trainers his age to despair?!!

Does he not care about being human at all? This is way too demoralizing, ugh ugh ugh...

"Emmm..."

Leah and Brock couldn't help but ponder. They were thinking, is it that we're too weak, or is there something wrong with Dylan, or maybe the world itself is broken?

After a few seconds of reflection, Leah and Brock were absolutely certain: there was nothing wrong with them because everyone else was just like them!

Leah even felt that she was actually stronger than most of her peers, alright? When it came to monsters like Red, Leah didn't even bother to compare—she had already said he was a monster, so there was no point in comparing.

As for Dylan...

Leah was sure of one thing: it's not her fault, it's Dylan's!

Who's responsible for making him such a freak?!?!

Yes, Leah was sure she hadn't lost her mind. The world must be the one going mad.

(End of Chapter)

# 46: Chapter 46: He’s Really So Sunshine-y

Dylan was unaware of what Leah and the others were thinking at the moment; his focus was entirely on the battle.

"Alright, Red, let me see your final Pokémon."

Dylan extended his hand with a smile.

Red's red eyes were calm and focused. After a moment of contemplation, he threw out his starter Pokémon.

"Charizard!"

"Roar~!"

With a roar, a powerful Charizard flew above Red, its wings beating as it soared through the air without landing. (In "Pokémon Origins," Red's starter is Charmander.)

"Looks like you're still cautious of Toxic Spikes."

Dylan chuckled lightly at this and wasn't too surprised. Charizard has flying abilities, but it's rare for Charizard to make an entrance in flight.

Red was evidently still wary of the Toxic Spikes. In the games, Toxic Spikes don't affect Poison, Steel, or Flying-type Pokémon, and they only take effect when Pokémon enter the field.

But that's just in the games. The fact that Paul's Drapion caused damage even when it threw Ash's Staraptor to the ground shows that, even for Flying-type Pokémon, touching the Toxic Spikes field upon landing still results in Toxic Spikes damage.

So, Toxic Spikes are really quite useful!

Dylan had initially guessed that Red would use Charizard or Venusaur. In reality, Venusaur would be the best choice under normal circumstances.

Because Venusaur wouldn't be affected by Toxic Spikes. But Red is not one to give up easily. Even if victory seems unlikely, he'll still fight for it.

Venusaur taking on three Poison-type Pokémon would be extremely difficult.

"Charizard, go for it!"

Seeing Charizard come out, Ash was very excited. This was his brother's starter Pokémon, and it could definitely turn the tide!

"Come out, Charmander." Ash even released his own Charmander, wanting it to watch and learn from Charizard.

"Charmander, pay close attention. You'll evolve into something like this someday!"

"Charr~" Charmander nodded vigorously, its bright eyes filled with admiration as it looked at the impressive and mighty Charizard.

"This is Red's last Pokémon."

Brock wasn't as optimistic as Ash. If Charizard couldn't turn the battle around, Red would lose this Gym battle.

On the battlefield.

"Charizard, use Flamethrower!"

Red's handsome face remained calm and focused as he gave Charizard the command.

Charizard opened its mouth wide, and Red's flames gathered inside. The next moment, a blazing beam of fire shot out.

"Cross Poison!" Dylan ordered Drapion to counterattack. He wanted to gauge the intensity of Charizard's attack first.

Drapion's pincers crossed, emitting a purple energy. As the pincers swung heavily, a cross-shaped blade of light met Charizard's attack.

Boom!

The Cross Poison and Flamethrower collided heavily in midair, creating a roaring gust of wind. However, it wasn't long before the Cross Poison was overwhelmed and directly smashed through.

The fiery beam continued its descent and struck Drapion directly.

Drapion roared in pain, the burning sensation from the flames was quite uncomfortable.

"Special Attack is very high."

Dylan confirmed this. Drapion's physical attack was quite good, but the Cross Poison was actually overwhelmed by Charizard's Flamethrower.

Both in terms of strength and level, this Charizard was exceptional.

"Continue with Flamethrower!" Red ordered with a deep voice.

If Charizard could take down Drapion without getting close, that would be ideal.

Drapion's condition wasn't great after this long battle.

"Rock Slide!"

Charizard unleashed another intense flame, but Dylan wouldn't sit idly by.

Drapion roared in fury, and giant rocks formed in the air, rapidly shooting towards Charizard.

Rock-type Rock Slide!

Moreover, the rocks were clearly aimed to avoid Charizard's Flamethrower, making Red's eyes narrow.

Dylan wanted Drapion to trade blows with Charizard!

"Charizard, dodge!" Red ordered. Charizard immediately ceased its Flamethrower and flapped its wings to evade the incoming Rock Slide attack.

Flamethrower was normal damage to Drapion, but with Charizard's Fire and Flying-type combination, it was four times weak to Rock-type moves!

How could this be a trade?

Red's Charizard was strong, but a Rock-type move with four times effectiveness was no joke. Taking a Rock Slide hit would be too costly.

A normal person wouldn't accept such a trade.

"Charizard, Dragon Rush!" Red decided to take a more risky close-range approach instead of continuing long-range attacks.

"Roar!"

Charizard let out a thunderous roar as pure Dragon-type energy coalesced around it.

In the next moment, this dense, rich energy transformed into a fearsome dragon, exuding a tremendous, intimidating aura.

Dragon-type Dragon Rush! It released a terrifying aura while both intimidating and charging at the opponent!

In the games, Dragon Rush is powerful but has a low accuracy rate, making it a move that often sits on the bench.

However, in reality, Dragon Rush's usage rate isn't low. Its imposing aura can lock and intimidate opponents, making its accuracy quite decent.

"This move is really cool." Dylan admired the fearsome dragon, genuinely impressed.

No matter what, Dragon Rush's special effects are incredibly impressive. That dragon made of energy is just so cool!

And real battles aren't turn-based. The outcome of special moves doesn't just depend on accuracy and luck.

The opponent isn't a target; unless you use a move that guarantees a hit, there's no such thing as a move that will definitely hit.

Even if you use a high-speed move that is guaranteed to hit, if you can't dodge it due to speed, you can still use a special move to counterattack directly.

"Retreat, use Pin Missile!"

Dylan ordered decisively.

Charizard's Dragon Rush was coming fast and furiously.

Drapion stepped back and raised its pincers to use Pin Missile in retaliation.

Boom boom boom!

Pin Missile continuously struck the attacking fearsome dragon, but all the Pin Missiles were shattered by Dragon Rush, barely managing to delay it for a bit.

But that time was not enough. The distance between them was closing rapidly!

"Toxic!" Dylan immediately ordered.

Drapion stopped, and the massive pincers on its back glowed a deep purple. It swung heavily, sending a Toxic attack at Charizard.

Boom~!

Toxic and Dragon Rush clashed head-on. Intense shockwaves burst forth, and the ground beneath Drapion cracked with fissures.

Drapion's face was extremely ferocious as it struggled to resist the silent-roaring dragon, but Dragon Rush was just too heavy!

Boom!

Finally, the fearsome dragon broke through the obstructing Toxic attack and slammed into Drapion, delivering a massive impact. Drapion roared in pain.

With a violent explosion, Charizard soared into the air.

"Flamethrower!" Red commanded without hesitation.

Charizard gathered flames in its mouth, and a scorching pillar of fire fell down.

Another explosion erupted, and thick smoke billowed.

As Charizard flapped its wings, a strong wind blew away the smoke that obscured the view.

Drapion's massive body lay unconscious on the ground.

"Drapion is unable to battle. Charizard wins!"

Leah stepped forward to check and announced the result.

"Ah~! Finally won!"

Ash, who had been watching, finally sighed in relief. From Pikachu to now Charizard, the pressure Drapion had brought was intense, even for an observer like Ash.

And now, it was finally defeated!

"Go, Red! Charizard, keep it up!"

Ash immediately cheered loudly for his brother, encouraging him to win the remaining two battles for victory!

"Boss is entering phase three."

Misty joked, genuinely curious about what Dylan's third Pokémon would be.

Under normal circumstances, the later a Pokémon is sent out, the stronger it is, right? But that's not always the case.

"Indeed, I'm looking forward to it as well," Brock responded with a smile. He was also curious about Dylan's next Pokémon.

"Do you guys seem to be supporting Dylan more?"

Ash looked at his two close travel companions with a strange expression.

On the field.

Dylan looked at the incapacitated Drapion, chuckling lightly as he recalled it.

"Well done, Red. Now, let's see your third opponent."

Dylan took out a Pokéball and without saying much, threw it.

"Toxapex! Prepare for battle!"

As the white light flashed, a Pokémon resembling a tent-like dome appeared in front of Dylan.

Then, under Ash's astonished gaze, the front two tentacles lifted and revealed its true form—a strange little doll with a purple body and yellow eyes.

This was the evolved form of Mareanie.

"This Pokémon looks so strange." Ash bit his finger, looking puzzled at the tentacles surrounding it.

Ash tried to look up the information using his Pokédex, but as expected, it had no data.

Ash put away the Pokédex with a frown. Damn! He definitely needed Professor Oak to update it!

"This is Toxapex, evolved from Mareanie," Ilene kindly handed her own Pokédex to Ash, so they could understand Toxapex's information.

'Ding~ Toxapex, Poison/Water-type, non-Star Pokémon

Ability: Merciless

Moves: Spikes, Water Pulse, Toxic Spikes, Toxic, Recover, Light Screen, Substitute, Iron Defense, Pain Split, Bad Dreams...

Description: If poisoned by Toxapex, one would suffer from intense pain for three days and nights. Even with treatment, it will leave aftereffects.'

Silence!

Seeing Toxapex's data, Leah, Misty, and Brock all had very strange expressions. It was the kind of expression where they wanted to say something but couldn't quite find the words.

"Hehe… these moves are really…"

In the end, Misty couldn't hold back and spoke with a dry laugh, but she genuinely didn't know how to describe it.

Isn't this guy just a psychological case taking revenge on society? He uses such Pokémon to deal with challengers. Isn't he afraid that challengers will challenge him to a real fight after the match?

Spikes, Toxic Spikes, Toxic, Recover, Light Screen, Iron Defense, and so on—good grief, there are hardly any offensive moves. It's all like this.

Considering these moves, Misty didn't even need to think. This is definitely a tumor-type Pokémon that Misty and Dylan share!

"He's really so sunshine-y~"

Misty genuinely exclaimed.

"Isn't he? Dylan has always been such a gentle person~" Ilene squinted her eyes and smiled, pleased that her childhood friend was recognized.

Leah and Brock wanted to say something but stopped. Misty's comment didn't quite convey what they meant!

But looking at Ilene's gentle and elegant demeanor, they didn't say anything, feeling quite uncomfortable!

(End of Chapter)

# 47: Chapter 47: Toxapex

"Isn't Toxapex a Poison and Water-type Pokémon?"

Red looked at Toxapex and recalled the information he had seen on the Pokémon League's official website. However, that website only showed the Pokémon's types and didn't provide detailed information.

Red had a great memory, so he quickly remembered Toxapex's types.

But he couldn't recall exactly what kind of Pokémon Toxapex was.

No matter—testing it out would be fine.

"That's right, Red. My Toxapex is quite tough to handle. It's one of my key players."

Dylan smiled as he spoke. He wasn't lying—Toxapex was indeed a crucial member of his current team.

Although it wasn't his ace, Dylan had to admit that his ace might not be as difficult to deal with as Toxapex.

"..."

Red's usually serene face grew serious.

Since Dylan said that, Toxapex must be very strong!

"Charizard, Air Slash!"

After a moment's hesitation, Red decided to make the first move to test things out.

Toxapex was a Water and Poison-type Pokémon, weak only to Ground, Electric, and Psychic types, and resistant to Fire-type moves.

However, among the three types that counter Toxapex, Charizard only knew Dig, which would inevitably make contact with the ground and thus be affected by Toxic Spikes.

Fortunately, Flying-type moves would deal normal damage to Toxapex.

Charizard flapped its wings heavily, and a powerful gust of wind formed an air blade that shot towards Toxapex.

"Light Screen," Dylan said with a faint smile, speaking slowly and calmly.

Toxapex lowered its two front tentacles, and a semi-transparent Light Screen materialized in front of it.

Light Screen is a Psychic-type move that reduces the damage from special attacks for a set period.

"..." Red frowned slightly.

The sharp Air Slash hit Toxapex's tentacles directly, causing Toxapex's body to shiver for a moment, but then…

Nothing else happened!

The Light Screen became more solid, eventually merging into Toxapex's body, completing the shield!

"What kind of defense is this? Did Air Slash not hit?" Ash stared, pointing at Toxapex in disbelief.

Air Slash hit, but Toxapex only shivered a bit and took the hit without much effect?

"Toxapex's physical and special defense are extremely high. When our Arbok and Drapion practice their moves, they use Toxapex as their target."

"Even their Earthquake doesn't deal much damage to Toxapex."

Ilene explained with a gentle smile. This way, both Arbok and Drapion could improve their move intensity and Toxapex's defense.

However, currently, the attack strength of Arbok and Drapion had long fallen behind Toxapex's defensive growth rate.

If Toxapex weren't so reserved, then if it were a Pokémon like Arbok, it would definitely say to Arbok and Drapion, "Is that all? Haven't you eaten? Put some effort in!"

"Huh?"

Ash was dumbfounded.

Red on the field had already furrowed his brows. Not only was the Air Slash's impact on Toxapex less than ideal, but with the Light Screen up, how could Charizard attack from a distance?

The Light Screen's reduction of special attack damage was too effective.

"Toxapex, Iron Defense."

Dylan's tone remained relaxed. He had great trust in his Toxapex, especially since Red didn't know much about it.

Toxapex let out a low hum, and a metallic sheen enveloped its body.

Iron Defense, a Steel-type move, greatly increases the user's physical defense.

"Dragon Rush!"

Red said solemnly.

If long-range attacks were ineffective, then close combat was the way to go! And he couldn't just watch his opponent strengthen.

Toxapex had already used Light Screen. If it used Iron Defense twice more, Charizard wouldn't be able to land a hit!

Roar~!

A furious dragon's roar filled the air as the powerful Dragon energy surged around Charizard, descending from the sky.

Dragon Rush!

Charizard was not only strong in special attacks but also excelled in close combat!

Dragon Rush's charging speed was fast, and it soon arrived above Toxapex.

"You've fallen for it, Red."

Dylan's lips curled up.

"Toxapex, Baneful Bunker!"

Toxapex immediately stopped using Iron Defense, and a strange purple barrier appeared around its body.

Baneful Bunker! This is Toxapex's exclusive move.

Baneful Bunker: Protects itself from most moves from other Pokémon.

Yes, it's essentially a Protect-type move, but unlike Protect, during Baneful Bunker, if an opponent uses a contact move against Toxapex, the attacker will be poisoned!

And contact moves generally include most close-range attacks, Dragon Rush being no exception.

"Wait! Charizard!" Red looked at the peculiar purple barrier with some unease.

But it was too late. Charizard was too close to Toxapex, and even if Charizard wanted to stop, it was already too late.

Boom!

The fierce dragon slammed into the Baneful Bunker shield, and a torrent of energy surged, creating a strong wind. The dragon roared frantically, trying to shatter the shield below.

But it was futile. Although the Baneful Bunker shield was thin, it was exceptionally sturdy.

As Dragon Rush's violent impact continued, a purple fluid from the Baneful Bunker flowed over the dragon, eventually seeping into Charizard's body.

Dragon Rush finally exhausted its power, and the Baneful Bunker shield dissipated into light particles. Charizard flapped its wings, trying to fly away.

But just then, a layer of purple poisonous light suddenly enveloped Charizard, making its body stiffen and its face contort in pain.

Red's pupils contracted. Poisoned?! Was it because of that strange purple Protect?

Wait, does your Protect carry poison? Is there such an excessive move?

Red fell silent.

And this scene also left the surrounding crowd stunned.

"Huh? How did Charizard get poisoned?"

"Baneful Bunker! It's that Baneful Bunker move!"

"Goodness, this move is too sneaky!"

"Sure enough, Pokémon with abstract appearances are always troublesome!"

"Guys, I suddenly really want to play a Poison-type team too!"

The spectators were either astonished, intrigued, or silent, but all were impacted in some way.

"Venoshock!"

Dylan didn't care about this. He immediately ordered Toxapex to pursue.

Toxapex raised its two tentacles, and purple poison gathered. Two columns of poisonous liquid shot out.

Poison-type, Venoshock!

Bang~!

The Venoshock struck Charizard, who was just recovering from the poison, right in the face.

That's right, Toxapex aimed the poison directly at Charizard's face!

Charizard roared in pain as its strong body toppled, crashing heavily to the ground.

The Merciless ability always targets critical hits on poisoned opponents, and Venoshock's power doubles against poisoned targets!

This means that this Venoshock hit two buffs—Charizard had no chance of escaping the pain!

And as Charizard hit the ground, it triggered another layer of Toxic Spikes, with purple poison quickly enveloping Charizard again.

"Hiss…"

Seeing this, Ash and the others couldn't help but gasp, and Charmander beside Ash stared blankly at its now miserable senior, its eyes wide with shock.

That's right, the damage from Toxic Spikes and poisoning does not overlap! Even if poisoned, the damage from Toxic Spikes still applies.

Because a single layer of Toxic Spikes does not cause true poisoning, only two layers will poison the opponent, something Dylan has experimented with.

In fact, compared to the two-layer Toxic Spikes poisoning, the three-layer Toxic Spikes with its toxic status, Dylan found a single layer of Toxic Spikes more practical.

Charizard was lying on the ground, eyes shut, face twisted, and body curled up. The pain from Venoshock and the subsequent impact had left it in agony.

Pain! It really hurt so much!

"Red has no hope left." Brock shook his head as he came to this realization.

Charizard was already poisoned, and with that series of attacks, Charizard's condition was critical.

And with Toxapex's ridiculous defense and Light Screen protecting it, even if it was a battle of attrition, Charizard would be worn out, not to mention Toxapex's self-recovery.

Red's defeat was certain.

"This guy is really strong…" Misty agreed, looking at Dylan with admiration.

Although there was already a Poison-type Gym in the Kanto region—Fuchsia City's Koga was a Poison-type expert.

Misty wondered who was better at using Poison-type Pokémon: Dylan or Koga.

Curious, Misty tilted her head, suddenly looking forward to a battle between these two Poison-type experts.

Hmm, that scene would definitely be very interesting.

(End of Chapter)

# 48: Chapter 48: Not be Poison-type

The battle between Red and Dylan had lost all suspense. Charizard was already poisoned, and Toxapex's defenses were simply too formidable.

Even though Red's Charizard was a dual-threat (strong in both physical and special attacks), it was of little use in this situation.

For Red, the remainder of the battle was essentially a form of torture; at this point, most people would have forfeited.

There was simply no chance of victory.

However, Red's willpower was indeed resilient. Despite the dire situation, he still didn't give up, searching for any opportunity.

Yet, relentless perseverance doesn't always yield rewards.

During another bout of poisoning, Charizard was once again struck down by Toxapex's venom and didn't rise this time.

"Charizard is unable to continue. Toxapex wins."

"Since the challenger, Red, has lost all four Pokémon, the battle is won by Gym Leader Dylan."

Leah waved a small flag to announce the result.

Upon hearing this, Red's crimson eyes flickered with a hint of dimness but quickly rekindled with intensity, staring eagerly at Dylan.

"Even Red lost…?"

Ash, who was watching, looked particularly dejected. His brother, whom he always admired and looked up to, had lost, and it made him feel quite upset.

Beside Ash, Charmander was also staring blankly at the fallen Charizard, unsure of what to think.

"No trainer can guarantee a 100% win rate, Ash."

Brock patted Ash's shoulder, smiling as he comforted him. No one is truly undefeated in battle.

"Even the champion at the top of the Pokémon League doesn't win every battle. Moreover, Red is still young, and—"

Brock glanced at Red with a complicated expression. Red was indeed an incredibly formidable rookie trainer.

But…

Brock then looked at Dylan.

"Dylan is indeed very strong, and his use of Poison-type Pokémon is exceptional. It's not surprising that Red lost. However, a key trait of a trainer is whether they can learn from their defeats and become stronger."

"Ash, do you think your brother is that kind of person?"

Brock asked suddenly.

"Of course!" Ash replied without hesitation.

"Then it's just as I thought." Brock smiled.

"But…"

"There's no but. These two are both monsters; it's not surprising who wins or loses."

Misty interrupted Ash with a dismissive wave. To her, both Dylan and Red were just different kinds of monsters, so the outcome didn't seem strange at all.

"By the way, do you think Dylan will give Red the Rota Badge?"

Misty was now more interested in this question.

"Huh?" Ash looked puzzled and scratched his head.

"Isn't it that a challenger has to defeat the Gym Leader to get the badge?" Ash questioned, not understanding.

A Gym Badge was supposed to be a trophy and proof obtained by defeating the Gym Leader, right?

"Who told you that you have to defeat the Gym Leader to get a badge?" Misty retorted.

Isn't that how it works?

Ash had three question marks floating above his head.

"Ash, Gym Leaders are facilities set up by the League to train and test trainers. Fundamentally, Gym battles are a kind of assessment."

Brock explained patiently. As someone who had been a Gym Leader at the Pewter Gym, he had a lot of say in this matter.

"The decision on whether to award a badge to a challenger is ultimately up to the Gym Leader. Defeating the Gym Leader is the most straightforward method, as it indirectly proves the challenger's strength."

"But sometimes, when a Gym Leader recognizes a challenger's abilities, they may choose to give the badge even if the challenger loses."

Brock described this in detail. He had given badges to a few promising newcomers in the past.

"Red's strength is beyond question. Even if he didn't defeat Dylan, Dylan could still choose to give Red the badge, but that decision is in Dylan's hands."

Brock smiled, not doubting that Red, even with his current strength, would have a very high chance of winning the Indigo Plateau Championship.

After all, both Dylan and Red had already defeated Team Rocket Executive Archer.

But some Gym Leaders require challengers to defeat them to earn the badge, so Brock and Misty weren't sure if Dylan would give Red the badge.

"I see." Ash understood.

"However…" Ash tilted his head, wondering if Red would accept the Rota Badge if Dylan offered it.

On the field.

"That was an excellent battle, Red. You are the first true strong opponent I've defeated."

"I'm very happy with this battle."

Dylan walked up to Red, extending his hand with a smile.

Of course, he was happy. He had enjoyed the battle thoroughly.

Next to Dylan was Toxapex, still shyly following Dylan just as it did in the past.

"Thank you, I gained a lot from this."

Red shook Dylan's hand with one hand, while using the other to adjust the brim of his cap, habitually shading his eyes.

"I will challenge you again!"

Red, rarely talkative outside of battle, spoke fervently. Even with his eyes in shadow, the intensity was palpable.

Although this Gym battle didn't go well, Red wasn't particularly concerned about the defeat. It wasn't the first time he had lost to a peer.

When he first left Pallet Town, he lost to his childhood rival, Blue.

But did it matter? Not at all. Over this time traveling, Red seemed to have a sort of fate with that arrogant guy, as they coincidentally crossed paths four times.

And each time, they inevitably battled. As of now, their record was two wins and two losses.

For Red, losing wasn't frightening. Losing to others only meant one thing:

Not strong enough!

Experiences of failure were invaluable and wouldn't break him but would instead make him stronger. Until he reaches the top of Kanto and the pinnacle of the world!

"I look forward to your next challenge."

Dylan gazed into Red's eyes, which seemed deeper due to the shadow of the cap, and his smile widened slightly.

Dylan then took out a small, exquisite box from his pocket, opening it to reveal a glittering, emerald-green tree-shaped badge. The badge was small yet beautiful, with a vibrant and eye-catching green color.

This was the Rota Gym Badge, The Tree of Beginning Badge.

"Even though you didn't defeat me, Red, you deserve this Rota Badge."

Dylan took out the Rota Badge and extended it to Red.

Dylan considered himself a competent Gym Leader. Although Red lost, Dylan knew that Red deserved this badge.

"…"

Red looked at the vibrant Rota Badge in silence, not reaching out to take it.

"This is the first badge I want to give away since my 'transformation,' Red."

Dylan expressed his goodwill. He wasn't using this action to mock Red but was sincere.

And this was the first badge Dylan wanted to give away after his reincarnation!

"This badge… I can't accept it." Red shook his head firmly.

"I will come to challenge you at your Gym next time, Dylan. I will defeat you fairly and earn your badge."

Red's youthful face was serious and earnest. This was the first time he spoke so much to someone he had just met.

And similarly, apart from his childhood friend, this was the first time he had been defeated by another peer!

"Oh?" Dylan raised an eyebrow.

As expected of siblings, although he guessed that Red might not accept the 'free' badge, it turned out that Red, like Ash, had the same 'unyielding' trait.

"Since you say so, I look forward to it. But Red, defeating me to earn my badge will be very difficult."

Dylan retracted the Rota Badge, teasingly adding with a smile.

Since Red had such determination, Dylan would satisfy it.

"Perhaps you'll never get my badge."

"Next time, I won't lose!"

Red said firmly.

After all, this was his first time battling such a strong Poison-type expert, and his experience was too limited.

With this loss as a valuable experience, Red believed that with sufficient preparation and strategy adjustments, he would win next time!

It's just Poison, right?

"Such touching confidence~"

Dylan seemed to praise and sigh at the same time. After all, to truly reach the top, how could one defeat an opponent without such unwavering determination?

"I will await your visit, but Red, next time, the team I use might not be Poison-type."

Dylan spoke slowly. Although he was increasingly fond of Poison-type Pokémon, he wouldn't stick only to them.

"??"

Red's red eyes filled with question marks.

What does that mean? Aren't you specialized in Poison-types? You actually have teams of other types?

(End of Chapter)

# 49: Chapter 49: The Artificial 'God'

Red remained silent, falling back into his usual brooding state. But perhaps it was for the best!

The more challenging the Rota Gym was, the greater the sense of accomplishment upon obtaining the Rota Badge, right? Seeing Red revert to his usual aloof demeanor, Dylan shrugged.

Perhaps in the future, Red would realize that despite having defeated the Elite Four of Kanto, vanquished his childhood rival Blue, and become the Champion of Kanto, he would look back and wonder why he still couldn't obtain the Rota Gym Badge.

Hmm, thinking about it, Dylan found it quite interesting.

"That was a fantastic battle. You two certainly lived up to expectations, Dylan, Red."

Temporary referee Leah, having seen the post-battle comments, cheerfully approached.

Today had been quite good—not only had they thwarted the plans of the detestable Team Rocket, but they had also met two impressive peers.

The battle had been enjoyable to watch as well. However, now that it was over, she needed to continue her journey.

"Well then, cute little Leah will take her leave now. Let's meet again if fate allows~"

With a playful wave and a cute blink of her large eyes, Leah said her goodbyes.

Next, Dylan and Red would need to tend to their injured Pokémon, and Leah had to continue her travels.

"..." Red.

"Goodbye. If you have time, come visit Rota," Dylan said with a smile.

"Alright~ Maybe if I can't get by out here, I'll come to apply for a position at your Gym."

Leah's pure smile was as bright as a sunflower, embodying a lively and optimistic young girl.

At least she appeared that way on the surface.

"You're always welcome. The benefits are good," Dylan couldn't help but smile.

"Mmhm~"

Leah smiled sweetly and skipped away with a light, joyful step.

Dylan, Red, and the others returned to the Pokémon Center, but with so many injured Pokémon from the battle, Nurse Joy was still quite busy.

Dylan and Ilene decided not to wait any longer; they needed to return to Rota tonight.

"Come visit Rota when you have time. As your host, I'll make sure to give you a warm welcome," Dylan said with a smile as he bid farewell to Red, Ash, and the others.

"We definitely will! Dylan, I'm going to challenge your Gym too!" Ash declared confidently, clenching his fist. He had decided that he would get the Rota Gym Badge!

Misty rolled her eyes, and Brock chuckled.

"Oh? Quite spirited," Dylan observed with interest.

"I'll give it my all too, Ash. I look forward to your visit."

Dylan was not discouraging the eager young boy but was smiling in response.

Though he had just begun his journey, Ash's enthusiasm as a challenger was quite interesting.

Speaking of which…

Tomorrow, Selene would also be coming to the Gym as an apprentice. As the teacher, Dylan was contemplating whether to prepare a starter Pokémon for this first apprentice.

After the challenge, Selene could also train with Ash.

After all, one was the main character from the anime, and the other was the protagonist of the seventh-generation game—both were key figures in the Pokémon series.

"By the way, Red, I recorded the battle between you and Dylan. May I post it online?"

Ilene remembered this and asked Red. As a queen, she naturally respected others' privacy, including Dylan's.

"...Yes."

Red paused at the question, then glanced at Dylan and nodded.

There was no reason not to; Red didn't feel embarrassed. He had lost honestly, and Dylan's use of Poison-type Pokémon was outstanding.

"Could I get a copy of the video?" Red asked hesitantly.

It would be best to have the video so he could review the battle in detail later. It would help him prepare for his next challenge, although Red didn't know if Dylan would use the same team next time.

"Of course," Ilene nodded with an elegant smile.

Ilene and Red exchanged social media contacts, and she would send the video to Red once they returned to Rota.

Misty and Brock also took the initiative to add Ilene as a friend; they were also interested in watching the battle video.

"Well then, goodbye everyone. Red, next time you challenge us, it will be an all-out battle."

Dylan waved goodbye to the four, finally focusing on Red.

"Sure," Red nodded seriously. A 6v6 full battle was undoubtedly the most challenging mode for evaluating a trainer's skills, tactics, and depth.

Red looked forward to it!

With that, Dylan and Ilene left the Pokémon Center.

As they exited the center, Ilene looked up at the sky, her beautiful eyes momentarily clouded.

"Today's experience feels... a bit surreal."

Could it not be surreal? She was originally a noble queen, had just come out for a brief vacation with her childhood friend, and then, by chance, boarded the world-class cruise ship S.S Anne.

And then she encountered the Kanto evil organization Team Rocket...

It was truly an experience she had never imagined in her life, but fortunately, everything was fine.

Thinking of this, Ilene couldn't help but glance at the young man beside her, her lips instinctively curling into a happy smile.

"What's up? Why are you looking at me like that all of a sudden?"

Dylan, noticing Ilene's gaze, asked curiously.

"Nothing, just thinking... it's really nice to have Dylan around~"

Ilene smiled gently and spoke softly.

"Having Ilene with me is also my fortune," Dylan replied, taken aback but then responded warmly.

The two shared a smile and continued walking.

However, just as they were about to leave Vermilion Town, they spotted an elderly bald man with a slightly hunched back.

"Grandpa Fuji?"

Dylan looked at the bald old man with surprise.

Indeed, this was Dr. Fuji, the person Team Rocket's Archer wanted to capture on the S.S. Anne.

However, after the S.S. Anne returned to the port, Dr. Fuji was taken away by Officer Jenny for some questions, and Dylan and the others had parted ways with him.

To their surprise, they encountered him again here.

"Oh, it's Dylan and you all."

Dr. Fuji turned around, a kind smile on his face.

"I didn't expect to run into you here. Actually, I wanted to properly thank you, Red, and that little girl."

Dr. Fuji expressed his gratitude, though he was one of the many 'victims' of the S.S. Anne incident and needed to provide a statement to the police.

Fortunately, Officer Jenny and the others did not know that Team Rocket's goal was partly to capture him, or he wouldn't have been out so soon.

Dr. Fuji had requested Dylan and the others not to tell Officer Jenny about this.

Facing the old man's request, Red and Leah remained silent.

"No need to be polite, Grandpa Fuji. Team Rocket is despicable. As a Gym Leader, it's my duty to help."

Dylan said.

"What a good child~ It's wonderful to have young people like you in the League~"

Dr. Fuji sincerely remarked. He was a kind-hearted old man, but…

Thinking about his previous actions, Dr. Fuji's gaze darkened.

Seeing the sudden change in the old man's demeanor, Ilene was puzzled. This elderly man seemed to have many secrets.

"Grandpa Fuji, actually, before we arrived at the room, we heard some sounds outside."

Dylan pondered for a moment and tentatively asked Dr. Fuji.

"Were you once a member of Team Rocket?"

Dr. Fuji fell silent, his once kindly face now clouded with pain and regret.

"If it's inconvenient, you don't have to say. I believe you are not like Team Rocket's evil members."

After waiting for a while, Dylan comforted him gently, seemingly sensing Dr. Fuji's reluctance to confront this part of his past.

Dr. Fuji pressed his lips tightly, staring at the ground in a daze, and after a long while, finally gave a bitter smile.

"Actually… I was invited by Team Rocket to participate in a genetic experiment."

Dr. Fuji's voice carried undeniable exhaustion.

"An experiment involving 'artificial god' and cloning genetic organisms."

Ilene gasped.

Artificial... god?!!

(End of Chapter)
